.
Then, boost the momenta in the
\mathcal{H},eR
frame to the lab frame with a boost along the
\hat{z}
-axis with velocity

\beta_{\text{lab}}=\frac{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(E_{\text{\tiny{CM}}},m_{e},m_{\mathcal{H}})/(2E_{\text{\tiny{CM}}})}{\sqrt{\left(\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}(E_{\text{\tiny{CM}}},m_{e},m_{\mathcal{H}})}{2E_{\text{\tiny{CM}}}}\right)^{2}+m_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}}\,.

(A.48)

Appendix B
Dark Photon Compton Tensor in Coherent Scattering

In this appendix, we will outline how the Compton tensors with one dark photon leg are obtained for coherent scattering.
Electromagnetically interacting scalar particles with finite size and charge
Ze
can be described by an effective Lagrangian, given in Refs.
39
;
70
.
Although these references are mainly focused on pions, the discussion holds in general, so also for an
effective field theory of scalar nuclei.
The Lagrangian reads,

\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}=(D_{\mu}^{f}\varPhi^{+})^{\dagger}D^{\mu}_{f}\varPhi^{+}-m_{\varPhi}^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+},

(B.1)

where the noncanonical covariant derivative is given by,

D_{\mu}^{f}\varPhi^{+}=\{\partial_{\mu}+ieZA_{\mu}+ie[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}]\}\varPhi^{+}.

(B.2)

The function
f
acts on the field strength tensor.
In particular,
f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})
acting on a plane wave yields
f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})e^{iqx}=f(q^{2})e^{iqx}
.
Moreover,
f(q^{2})
is connected to the form factor
F(q^{2})
by,

f(q^{2})=\frac{F(q^{2})-Z}{q^{2}}.

(B.3)

Expanding the Lagrangian gives:

\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{0}

\displaystyle=(D_{\mu}\varPhi^{+})^{\dagger}D^{\mu}\varPhi^{+}-m_{\varPhi}^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+},

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{1}

\displaystyle=ie(D_{\mu}\varPhi^{+})^{\dagger}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}]-ie\varPhi^{-}D_{\mu}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}]^{\dagger},

(B.5)

\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{2}

\displaystyle=e^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}][f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\rho}F^{\mu\rho}]^{\dagger},

(B.6)

with the canonical covariant derivative
D_{\mu}\varPhi^{+}=(\partial_{\mu}+ieZA_{\mu})\varPhi^{+}
.
While
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{0}
is the usual scalar QED Lagrangian,
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{1}
and
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{2}
encode the finite size effects.
In particular,
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{0}
and
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{1}
together yield the electromagnetic vertex,

}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.46 0 L 36.7 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=ieF(q^{2})\left(2P^{\nu}-q^{\nu}\right)-ieq^{\mu}(P^{2}-{P^{\prime}}^{2})f(q^{2}),

(B.7)

which satisfies the Ward-Takahashi identity. For on-shell external
\varPhi^{+}
, this reduces to Eq. (
2.19
).
Now, expanding out the QED interactions and performing the substitution
A_{\mu}\mapsto A_{\mu}^{\text{\tiny{SM}}}\approx A_{\mu}-\varepsilon A_{\mu}^{\prime}
will yield the effective low-energy dark photon interactions.

The interaction terms linear in the photon field
A_{\mu}^{\text{\tiny{SM}}}
are straightforward. They yield the same interactions involving
A^{\prime}
, but with the coupling rescaled by
-\varepsilon
, thus giving Eq. (
2.20
).

The interaction terms quadratic
A_{\mu}^{\text{\tiny{SM}}}
are more involved.
Imposing
A_{\mu}^{\text{\tiny{SM}}}\approx A_{\mu}-\varepsilon A_{\mu}^{\prime}
results in terms
\propto A_{\mu}A^{\mu}
,
\propto A_{\mu}A^{\prime\,\mu}
and
\propto A^{\prime}_{\mu}A^{\prime\,\mu}
.
While the
A_{\mu}A^{\mu}
terms contribute to the QED Compton tensor with a symmetry factor of
2
, we find the mixed terms
\propto A_{\mu}A^{\prime\,\mu}
to appear always twice.
Thus, the contribution from
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{0}+\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{1}
to the Compton tensor with one dark photon leg is identical to the pure QED Compton tensor, except for the
-\varepsilon
rescaling.
The contribution to the QED Compton tensor from
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{2}
vanishes if at least one photon is real, satisfying
\partial_{\nu}F^{\mu\nu}=0
.
This is not true in the case of the dark photon, since it satisfies the Proca equation,
\partial_{\nu}F^{\prime\,\mu\nu}+m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}A^{\prime\,\mu}=0
.
In presence of a dark photon,
\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{2}
becomes:

\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{2}

\displaystyle=e^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}][f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\rho}F^{\mu\rho}]^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon e^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial^{\nu}F^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}][f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\rho}F^{\mu\rho}]^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon e^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}][f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\rho}F^{\prime\,\mu\rho}]^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon^{2}e^{2}\varPhi^{-}\varPhi^{+}[f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial^{\nu}F^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}][f(-\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}})\partial_{\rho}F^{\prime\,\mu\rho}]^{\dagger}.

(B.8)

(B.9)

such that we end up with Eq. (
2.22
) in total.

Appendix C
Dark Goldstone Boson in
R_{\xi}
and Feynman Gauge

Loop corrections are most commonly carried out in Feynman-t’Hooft gauge, where the Goldstone bosons associated to the massive vector bosons are propagating degrees of freedom.
To calculate the loop corrections involving the dark photon, we will thus rephrase the commonly used effective theory below the electroweak scale to explicitly contain the Goldstone boson and a gauge parameter that controls its presence.
For physics applications below the electroweak scale, it is sufficient to use kinetic mixing of the new, heavy
U(1)_{\text{\tiny{D}}}
gauge boson with the QED photon.
Let the
U(1)_{\text{\tiny{D}}}
gauge field be
A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}
with field strength tensor
F_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu\nu}=\partial^{\mu}A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\nu}-\partial^{\nu}A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}
and mass
m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}
.
The dark sector Lagrangian in which the
U(1)_{\text{\tiny{D}}}
gauge symmetry is spontaneously broken by an abelian Higgs mechanism then contains,

\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{\text{\tiny{D}}}\supset-\frac{1}{4}{F_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}_{\mu\nu}{F_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}^{2}\left[A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}}\partial^{\mu}\pi\right]^{2}\,,

(C.1)

where
\pi
is the Goldstone boson associated to
A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}
.
The portal interaction is the kinetic mixing of
A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}
(dark sector) and
A_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}^{\mu}
(SM photon),

\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{\text{\tiny{SM}}\otimes\text{\tiny{D}}}=-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}{F_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}}_{\mu\nu}F_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu\nu}=-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\>\Big[\partial_{\mu}{A_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}}_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}{A_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}}_{\mu}\Big]\>\left[\partial^{\mu}A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\nu}-\partial^{\nu}A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}\right]\,.

(C.2)

Now, to achieve a consistent treatment, we will modify the field strength tensor to also contain the Goldstone field
\pi
.
In light of the form of the mass term in Eq. (
C.1
), we write

\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}_{\text{\tiny{SM}}\otimes\text{\tiny{D}}}=-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big[\partial_{\mu}{A_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}}_{\mu}-\partial_{\nu}{A_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}}_{\mu}\Big]\>\left[\partial^{\mu}\left(A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\nu}+\frac{1}{m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}}\partial^{\nu}\pi\right)-\partial^{\nu}\left(A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}}\partial^{\mu}\pi\right)\right]\,.

(C.3)

Note that substituting
A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}\mapsto A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}+m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}^{-1}\partial^{\mu}\pi
behaves here exactly like a gauge transformation:
Since the derivatives commute, the field strength tensor is unchanged and thus the kinetic mixing operator.
In the abelian Higgs mechanism, the Goldstone field is conveniently removed by performing a gauge fixing of this type.
The next step is to rotate away the kinetic mixing of the two photons.
This is achieved with the transformation,

\begin{pmatrix}A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}}\partial^{\mu}\pi\\
A_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}&0\\
-\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{{A^{\prime}}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{{A^{\prime}}}}\partial^{\mu}\pi\\
A^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(C.4)

The field strength tensors then become

\displaystyle F_{\text{\tiny{D}}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}{F^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu},

(C.5)

\displaystyle F_{\text{\tiny{SM}}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\mu}\left[\frac{-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}\left(A^{\prime\,\nu}+\frac{1}{m_{A^{\prime}}}\partial^{\nu}\pi\right)+A^{\nu}\right]-\partial^{\nu}\left[\frac{-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}\left(A^{\prime\,\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A^{\prime}}}\partial^{\mu}\pi\right)+A^{\mu}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}F^{\prime\,\mu\nu}+F^{\mu\nu}.

(C.6)

Thus, the kinetic mixing among the photons is removed and the Lagrangian contains,

1 0 -.45 1
\mathscr{L}

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{1}{4}F^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}F^{\prime\,\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}^{2}}{1-\varepsilon^{2}}\left[A^{\prime}_{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A^{\prime}}}\partial^{\mu}\pi\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-J^{\mu}\left(\frac{-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}\left(A^{\prime}_{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A^{\prime}}}\partial_{\mu}\pi\right)+A_{\mu}\right)\,.

(C.7)

Here, we can identify
m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}=m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}^{2}/(1-\varepsilon^{2})
and obtain a coupling of the QED current
J^{\mu}
to the mass eigenstate
A^{\prime}
, which we call ‘dark photon’.
As last step, we can now perform the gauge fixing for the new spin-1 field. We introduce a gauge fixing parameter
\xi^{\prime}
and add the gauge-fixing term to the Lagrangian, which removes the kinetic mixing of
A^{\prime}
with
\pi
,

\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}\supset-\frac{1}{4}F^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}F^{\prime\,\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{A_{\text{\tiny{D}}}}^{2}}{1-\varepsilon^{2}}\left[A^{\prime}_{\mu}+\frac{1}{m_{A^{\prime}}}\partial_{\mu}\pi\right]^{2}-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}}\left(\partial_{\mu}A^{\prime\,\mu}-\xi^{\prime}m_{A^{\prime}}\pi\right)^{2}\,.

(C.8)

The propagators for the BSM fields thus are,

\mu
\nu

\displaystyle\sim~\frac{i}{p^{2}-m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}}\left(-g_{\mu\nu}+\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}-\xi^{\prime}m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}}(1-\xi^{\prime})\right)\,,

(C.9)

\displaystyle\sim~\frac{i}{p^{2}-\xi^{\prime}m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}}\,,

(C.10)

and with
J^{\mu}=eQ_{\ell}\bar{\ell}\gamma^{\mu}\ell
we get for the interaction vertices,

\ell
\ell
\mu

\displaystyle\sim~-ieQ_{\ell}\gamma^{\mu}\frac{-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}~\approx~ieQ_{\ell}\varepsilon\gamma^{\mu}\,,

(C.11)

\ell
\ell
k

\displaystyle\sim~-ieQ_{\ell}\gamma^{\mu}\frac{-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{1-\varepsilon^{2}}}\frac{-ik_{\mu}}{m_{A^{\prime}}}~\approx~\frac{eQ_{\ell}\varepsilon\not{k}}{m_{A^{\prime}}}\,,

(C.12)

where
e=\sqrt{4\pi\alpha}
, and the approximation
\varepsilon\ll 1
was applied.
In Feynman-t’Hooft gauge,
\xi^{\prime}\rightarrow 1
, we now indeed have the Goldstone as propagating degree of freedom.
For
\xi^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty
we are back in unitary gauge, where the Goldstone boson becomes infinitely heavy and thus decouples, while the dark photon propagator gets its longitudinal term
\propto p_{\mu}p_{\nu}/m_{{A^{\prime}}}^{2}
.

Appendix D
Renormalization and Counterterms

In this appendix, we present the key results of the renormalization procedure necessary to evaluate the NLO interference terms involving a leptophilic dark photon, see Eq. (
4.6
).
We consider a collection of fermions (the SM charged leptons) which are charged under U
(1)_{\text{\tiny{EM}}}
and receive an additional coupling to a massive dark photon, generated through the kinetic mixing operator.
Since LDMX-like experiments use beam energies well below the electroweak scale, we work in a theory without
W^{\pm}
and
Z^{0}
bosons and assume that the dark photon couples to left- and right-chiral fermions in the same way.

Our treatment is similar to the techniques presented in Ref.
32
:
we rephrase the Lagrangian to include counterterms in renormalized perturbation theory and determine the counterterms in the physical on-shell scheme by imposing appropriate renormalization conditions.

D.1
Parameter and Field Renormalization

First, we express the bare parameters and fields in terms of renormalized quantities.
The bare quantities will carry a subscript
0
, while the renormalized ones have no subscript.
The rescaling factors are denoted by the letter
Z
with appropiate subscripts and are then expanded in terms of counterterms
\delta Z
at the one loop level.
The renormalized parameters are the electric charge, the dark photon mass and the fermion masses, so we write

\displaystyle e_{0}

\displaystyle=Z_{e}e=(1+\delta Z_{e})e

(D.1)

\displaystyle m^{2}_{{A^{\prime}},0}

\displaystyle=Z_{m_{A^{\prime}}}m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}=\left(1+\delta Z_{m_{A^{\prime}}}\right)m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}=m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}+\delta m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}

(D.2)

\displaystyle m_{\ell,i,0}

\displaystyle=Z_{m_{\ell,i}}m_{\ell,i}=\left(1+\delta Z_{m_{\ell,i}}\right)m_{\ell,i}=m_{\ell,i}+\delta m_{\ell,i}\,.

(D.3)

The vector bosons mix at one loop. So we define the field strength renormalizations in the vector boson sector as,

\begin{pmatrix}{A^{\prime}}^{0}\\
A^{0}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}Z^{1/2}_{{A^{\prime}}{A^{\prime}}}&Z^{1/2}_{{A^{\prime}}A}\\
Z^{1/2}_{A{A^{\prime}}}&Z^{1/2}_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{A^{\prime}}\\
A\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1+\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}{A^{\prime}}}&\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}A}\\
\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{A{A^{\prime}}}&1+\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{AA}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{A^{\prime}}\\
A\end{pmatrix}\,.

(D.4)

and in the fermion sector simply as,

\displaystyle\ell_{i,0}

\displaystyle=\left[1+\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{i}^{\ell}\right]\ell_{i}\,.

(D.5)

D.2
Renormalization of the Vector Boson Sector

To obtain the
\delta Z_{A^{\prime}A}
and
\delta Z_{AA^{\prime}}
counter terms, we expand the vector boson kinetic terms and the dark photon mass term in terms of the renormalized fields and the counterterms. We find,

\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}\supset

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F^{0}_{\mu\nu}F_{0}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}{F^{\prime}}^{0}_{\mu\nu}{F^{\prime}}_{0}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}m^{2}_{{A^{\prime}},0}{A^{\prime}}^{0}_{\mu}{A^{\prime}}_{0}^{\mu}

(D.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\left(A_{\mu}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}}A^{\mu}-A_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\mu}A^{\nu}\right)(1+\delta Z_{AA})+\frac{1}{2}\left({A^{\prime}}_{\mu}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}}{A^{\prime}}^{\mu}-{A^{\prime}}_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\mu}{A^{\prime}}^{\nu}\right)(1+\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}{A^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle+\left(A_{\mu}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\textstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[7.7778pt]{\hbox to6.75pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to6.75pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=6.75pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[6.25002pt]{\hbox to5.20834pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to5.20834pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=5.20834pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3\makebox[5.41673pt]{\hbox to4.09743pt{\vrule width=6.77002pt\kern-6.77002pt\vbox to4.09743pt{\hrule height=6.77002pt,width=4.09743pt\vss\hrule height=13.54004pt}\kern-16.92505pt\vrule width=16.92505pt}}}}{A^{\prime}}^{\mu}-A_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial^{\mu}{A^{\prime}}^{\nu}\right)\frac{\delta Z_{A{A^{\prime}}}+\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}A}}{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}{A^{\prime}}_{\mu}{A^{\prime}}^{\mu}(1+\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}{A^{\prime}}})-{m_{A^{\prime}}}^{2}{A^{\prime}}_{\mu}A^{\mu}\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}A}-\frac{1}{2}\delta m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}{A^{\prime}}_{\mu}{A^{\prime}}^{\mu}\,,

(D.7)

where we neglected contributions quadratic in the counterterms. The relevant diagram involving the renormalisation constants
\delta Z_{A^{\prime}A}
and
\delta Z_{AA^{\prime}}
gives:

A,\mu
{A^{\prime}},\nu

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\delta Z_{A{A^{\prime}}}+\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}A}}{2}\left(p^{2}g_{\mu\nu}-p_{\mu}p_{\nu}\right)+ig_{\mu\nu}\frac{\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}A}}{2}m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}.

(D.8)

As a renormalization condition, we demand that the propagator matrix (derived from the vector boson self-energies and their one-loop mixing) is diagonal at both the photon and dark photon poles.
Explicitly, we then get for the two relevant counterterms:

\displaystyle\delta Z_{AA^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\frac{2\alpha\varepsilon Q_{\ell_{i}}^{2}}{9\pi m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}}\Bigg\{m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}-6m_{\ell_{i}}^{2}+6A_{0}(m_{\ell_{i}}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-3\left(m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}+2m_{\ell_{i}}^{2}\right)B_{0}(m_{A^{\prime}}^{2};m_{\ell_{i}}^{2},m_{\ell_{i}}^{2})\Bigg\}\,,

(D.9)

\displaystyle\delta Z_{A^{\prime}A}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(D.10)

where
A_{0}(m^{2})
and
B_{0}(p^{2};m_{a}^{2},m_{b}^{2})
are the usual Passarino-Veltman loop functions.

D.3
Vertex Renormalization

The fermion-photon vertex counterterm
\delta Z_{1}^{i}
is formally defined through the interaction terms.
Reexpressing the unrenormalized interaction Lagrangian in terms of renormalized quantities and counterterms gives,

\displaystyle\mbox{\hskip 6.25002pt\hskip 1.40623pt{1 0 -.45 1}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\mathscr{L}$}}\hskip-1.40623pt}\supset

\displaystyle-e_{0}Q_{\ell}\bar{\ell}_{i,0}\gamma^{\mu}\ell_{i,0}A_{\mu}^{0}-e_{0}Q_{\ell}\varepsilon\bar{\ell}_{i,0}\gamma^{\mu}\ell_{i,0}{{A^{\prime}}}^{0}_{\mu}

(D.11)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle-\left(1+\delta Z_{e}+\delta Z_{i}^{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{AA}+\varepsilon\frac{1}{2}\delta Z_{{A^{\prime}}A}\right)eQ_{\ell}\bar{\ell}_{i}\gamma^{\mu}\ell_{i}A_{\mu}

(D.12)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\left(1+\delta Z_{1}^{i}\right)eQ_{\ell}\bar{\ell}_{i}\gamma^{\mu}\ell_{i}A_{\mu},

(D.13)

where we again neglected contributions quadratic in the counterterms and implcitly defined
\delta Z_{1}^{i}
in the last step. So the counterterm Feynman rule simply is,

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\quad\sim\quad-ieQ_{\ell}\delta Z_{1}^{i}\,\gamma^{\mu}.

(D.14)

Instead of obtaining
\delta Z_{1}^{i}
from imposing the Thomson limit on the vertex function, we found it more convenient to use the relation implied by the Ward-Takahashi identity,
i.e.

\delta Z_{1}^{i}=\delta Z_{i}^{\ell},

(D.15)

which relates the vertex counterterm with the fermion wave function renormalization counterterm.
The latter is straightforwardly derived from the fermion self-energy function. The fermionic kinetic term is expanded as,

1 0 -.45 1
\mathscr{L}

\displaystyle\supset\bar{\ell}_{i}^{0}\left(i\not{\partial}-m_{\ell,i}^{0}\right)\ell_{i}^{0}

(D.16)

\displaystyle=\bar{\ell}_{i}\left(i\not{\partial}-m_{\ell,i}\right)\ell_{i}(1+\delta Z_{i}^{\ell})-\bar{\ell}_{i}\delta m_{\ell,i}\ell_{i}\,,

(D.17)

such that the counterterm will give:

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=i\left[\left(\not{p}-m_{\ell,i}\right)\delta Z_{i}^{\ell}-\delta m_{\ell,i}\right]\,.

(D.18)

The wave function renormalization counterterm is obtained from demanding that the propagator resulting form the 1-loop self-energy function has residue
i
.
Explicitly we find for the BSM part
\propto\varepsilon^{2}
,

\displaystyle\delta Z_{i}^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha\varepsilon^{2}Q_{\ell_{i}}^{2}}{4\pi m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}m_{\ell,i}^{2}}\Bigg\{\left(m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}+m_{\ell,i}^{2}\right)A_{0}(m_{A^{\prime}}^{2})-m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}A_{0}(m_{\ell,i}^{2})-m_{A^{\prime}}^{4}B_{0}(m_{\ell,i}^{2};m_{A^{\prime}}^{2},m_{\ell,i}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+m_{\ell,i}^{2}m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}\left[1+2\left(m_{A^{\prime}}^{2}+2m_{\ell,i}^{2}\right)B_{0}^{\prime}(m_{\ell,i}^{2};m_{A^{\prime}}^{2},m_{\ell,i}^{2})\right]\Bigg\}\,,

(D.19)

where
A_{0}(m^{2})
and
B_{0}(p^{2};m_{a}^{2},m_{b}^{2})
are again the Passarino-Veltman loop functions and
B_{0}^{\prime}(p^{2};m_{a}^{2},m_{b}^{2})=\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{2}}B_{0}(p^{2};m_{a}^{2},m_{b}^{2})
.

Appendix E
Supplementary Figures

Fig.
14
shows the double differential cross section,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma}{\mathrm{d}\xi_{e}\mathrm{d}\varOmega_{e}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{\text{LO}}}{\mathrm{d}\xi_{e}\mathrm{d}\varOmega_{e}}(e^{-}\varPhi^{+}\rightarrow e^{-}\varPhi^{+};\alpha^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{\text{NLO, real}}}{\mathrm{d}\xi_{e}\mathrm{d}\varOmega_{e}}(e^{-}\varPhi^{+}\rightarrow e^{-}\varPhi^{+}A^{\prime};\alpha^{3}\varepsilon^{2})+\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\sigma_{\text{NLO, virt}}}{\mathrm{d}\xi_{e}\mathrm{d}\varOmega_{e}}(e^{-}\varPhi^{+}\rightarrow e^{-}\varPhi^{+};\alpha^{3}\varepsilon^{2}).

(E.1)

for coherent scattering with a beam energy of
E=$3.2\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
.
This may be compared to Fig.
9
which shows the combination of coherent and diffractive scattering.

Figure 14:
Double differential cross section w.r.t. the energy fraction
\xi_{e}
and solid angle
\varOmega_{e}
of the recoiling electron taking into account
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{2})
LO and
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{3}\varepsilon^{2})
NLO contributions in coherent scattering. We set a beam energy of
E=$3.2\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}$
and show two benchmark points defined by different choices of dark photon mass
m_{A^{\prime}}
and squared kinetic mixing strength
\varepsilon
.

Appendix F
Supplementary Tables

The numerical evaluation of the nucleon form factors,
F_{i}^{N}(q^{2})
with
i=1,2
and
N=p,n
, requires the input of several parameters, cf. Sec.
2.2.2
.
In this appendix, we explicitly state the parameter values used for the calculations presented in this work.

For space-like arguments,
q^{2}<0
, we use the Kelly parameterization of the Sachs form factors,
cf.
Eqs. (
2.36
)–(
2.39
).
Table
5
shows the parameter values, taken from
58
.
Furthermore, for the nucleon magnetic moments we use the values:

\displaystyle\mu_{p}

\displaystyle=2.7928473446\,\mu_{N}\,,

(F.1)

\displaystyle\mu_{n}

\displaystyle=-1.9130427\,\mu_{N}\,,

(F.2)

with the nuclear magneton
\mu_{N}=3.15245125844

71
.

For time-like arguments,
q^{2}>0
we use the parameterization following from the vector meson dominance approach, which is reviewed in Ref.
60
,
cf.
also Eqs. (
2.40
)–(
2.43
).
We choose to adopt Model 1 from Ref.
60
in which the masses and widths of the highest resonances
\omega^{\prime\prime},\rho^{\prime\prime},\phi^{\prime\prime}
are varied around their PDG values, while the parameters of the lower resonances
\omega,\omega^{\prime},\rho,\rho^{\prime},\phi,\phi^{\prime}
remain fixed to their PDG values.
Table
6
shows the parameter values entering our calculations.
